# MAUI Proven Memory Leak Catalog

Last updated: 2026-06-29

## Catalog (362 Proven Or Upstream-Tracked Leaks)

| ID | Leak short description | Plain-language summary | Affected platform(s) | Retention targets / proof signal | Retention behavior | Trigger likelihood | Proof repro | Filed MAUI issue |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C001 | `WebView.Source` retains `WebView` through shared `WebViewSource.SourceChanged` | Occurs when `WebView` uses shared `WebViewSource.SourceChanged` that outlives it. Retains `WebView` until the long-lived source is cleared/replaced or the subscriber unsubscribes. Work around by clearing that property/source, unsubscribing, or avoiding long-lived shared publishers before disposal/navigation. | Cross-platform/Core | Retains WebView | Unbounded per subscribed control/page while the shared publisher lives. | Specific feature path; common in apps using the affected web/auth control or option. | [repro/webviewsource-leak](https://github.com/AdamEssenmacher/maui/tree/repro/webviewsource-leak) | [#35483](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/issues/35483) |
| C002 | `SelectionList` retains `SelectableItemsView` through external `SelectedItems.CollectionChanged` | Occurs when `SelectableItemsView` uses external `SelectedItems.CollectionChanged` that outlives it. Retains `SelectableItemsView` until the long-lived source is cleared/replaced or the subscriber unsubscribes. Work around by clearing that property/source, unsubscribing, or avoiding long-lived shared publishers before disposal/navigation. | Cross-platform/Core | Retains SelectableItemsView | Unbounded per subscribed control/page while the shared publisher lives. | Specific usage path documented by the repro. | [repro/selecteditems-selectionlist-leak](https://github.com/AdamEssenmacher/maui/tree/repro/selecteditems-selectionlist-leak) | [#35497](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/issues/35497) |
| C003 | iOS `CarouselViewController2` orientation notification observer is not stored/disposed | Occurs when iOS `CarouselViewController2` orientation notification observer is not stored/disposed. Retains `CarouselViewController2` observer/controller graphs until the observer, event, trigger, or listener is detached/cleared. Work around by detaching triggers, handlers, observers, or listeners before replacement/navigation when possible. | iOS/Mac Catalyst | iOS CarouselViewController2 orientation notification observer is not stored/disposed | Unbounded per disconnected/replaced native peer or handler while the retaining native/app root survives. | Regular in dynamic UIs that replace templates/content/header/footer/items or disconnect handlers. | [repro/carousel-view-2-observer-leak](https://github.com/AdamEssenmacher/maui/tree/repro/carousel-view-2-observer-leak) | [#35472](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/issues/35472) |

Fixed upstream by PR `#29837`, which introduced `WeakCommandSubscription` for core `ICommandElement` controls. Work around on affected builds by using MAUI `Command`/weak-event command implementations or clearing command properties before disposal/navigation. | Cross-platform Controls | Button/ImageButton/MenuItem/RefreshView/SearchBar/SearchHandler/TextCell command elements, custom long-lived commands, `CanExecuteChanged` handlers | Unbounded per subscribed control while the command lives on affected builds | Regular for apps using static/shared view models or reference-bound commands | [#29837](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/pull/29837) | [#29837](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/pull/29837) |
| M099 | Android foreground-service app relaunch kept old Windows/Window scopes alive because `Window.Destroying()` and alert cleanup did not run | Occurred when an Android app used a foreground service and the Activity was destroyed while the process stayed alive. `Window.Destroying()` was missed in the Activity destroy path and `AlertManager`/MessagingCenter roots could keep old `Window` and scoped service instances alive after relaunch. Fixed upstream by PR `#16045` and backport `#16240`, which call `Destroying()` from `OnDestroy()` and unsubscribe alert manager state during window destruction. Work around on affected builds by avoiding foreground-service process retention across Activity destruction or explicitly tearing down window/alert state. | Android | Old `Window`, scoped services, `AlertManager.AlertRequestHelper`, MessagingCenter subscriptions, Activity/window lifecycle state | Process lifetime across Activity destroy/relaunch when a service keeps the app alive | Specific to foreground-service/background lifecycle apps, but severe when triggered | [#15972](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/issues/15972)<br>[#16045](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/pull/16045)<br>[#16240](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/pull/16240) | [#15972](https://github.com/dotnet/maui/issues/15972) |


## Triage Matrix

These populated triage fields are inferred from the tracker proof, repro branch, source path, and platform notes. Treat them as a first-pass prioritization aid; exact fix risk and workaround quality should be revisited when a specific fix is designed.

Retained MiB values in this matrix are synthetic proof payloads. They demonstrate that a leak can retain realistic-sized objects, but they are not directly comparable across repros because each repro chooses payload size and iteration count to isolate a specific root. Use the MiB value as proof scale only, then prioritize by root lifetime, trigger likelihood, affected surface, native/resource pressure, and workaround quality.

Priority grades are first-pass triage urgency. GREF/JavaObject pressure and natural high-volume triggers such as scrolling, virtualization, image churn, and navigation loops intentionally raise priority; synthetic retained MiB values do not directly determine the grade.

Priority distribution: P0=0, P1=68, P2=215, P3=75, P4=2, P5=2.

| ID | Priority | Impact / proof evidence | Root lifetime | Recoverability | Workaround quality | Fix scope / risk | Likely user symptom |

Prioritize with root lifetime, trigger likelihood, affected surface, and native/resource involvement. | Shared publisher/event-source root; controls/pages stay reachable through strong event or command subscriptions. | Recovered by clearing/replacing the shared publisher or unsubscribing before the subscriber should die. | Public workaround: clear the property, unsubscribe, or avoid long-lived shared publishers before disposing/navigating away. | Image/media result ownership; medium-high risk due native resource disposal contracts. | Pages/view models fail to collect after navigation or source reuse; heap grows silently. |
